					<div id="mw-content-text" class="mw-body-content mw-content-ltr" lang="en" dir="ltr"><div class="mw-parser-output"><div class="shortdescription nomobile noexcerpt noprint searchaux" style="display:none">Empress of Russia from 1894 to 1917</div>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1033289096">.mw-parser-output .hatnote{font-style:italic}.mw-parser-output div.hatnote{padding-left:1.6em;margin-bottom:0.5em}.mw-parser-output .hatnote i{font-style:normal}.mw-parser-output .hatnote+link+.hatnote{margin-top:-0.5em}</style><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">This article is about the wife of Nicholas II of Russia. For Alexandra Feodorovna (1798–1860), the wife of Nicholas I, see <a href="/wiki/Alexandra_Feodorovna_(Charlotte_of_Prussia)" title="Alexandra Feodorovna (Charlotte of Prussia)">Alexandra Feodorovna (Charlotte of Prussia)</a>.</div>

<p><b>Alexandra Feodorovna</b> (<a href="/wiki/Russian_language" title="Russian language">Russian</a>: <span lang="ru">Александра Фёдоровна</span>; 6 June&#160;&#91;<a href="/wiki/Old_Style_and_New_Style_dates" title="Old Style and New Style dates">O.S.</a> 25 May&#93;&#160;1872 – 17 July 1918), <b>Princess Alix of Hesse and by Rhine</b> at birth,  was the last <a href="/wiki/List_of_Russian_royal_consorts" title="List of Russian royal consorts">Empress of Russia</a> as the consort of <a href="/wiki/Nicholas_II_of_Russia" title="Nicholas II of Russia">Emperor Nicholas II</a> from <a href="/wiki/Wedding_of_Nicholas_II_and_Alexandra_Feodorovna" title="Wedding of Nicholas II and Alexandra Feodorovna">their marriage</a> on 26 November&#160;&#91;<a href="/wiki/Old_Style_and_New_Style_dates" title="Old Style and New Style dates">O.S.</a> 14 November&#93;&#160;1894 until <a href="/wiki/Abdication_of_Nicholas_II" title="Abdication of Nicholas II">his forced abdication</a> on 15 March&#160;&#91;<a href="/wiki/Old_Style_and_New_Style_dates" title="Old Style and New Style dates">O.S.</a> 2 March&#93;&#160;1917. A favourite granddaughter of <a href="/wiki/Queen_Victoria" title="Queen Victoria">Queen Victoria of the United Kingdom</a>, she was, like her grandmother, one of the most famous <a href="/wiki/Haemophilia_in_European_royalty" title="Haemophilia in European royalty">royal carriers of haemophilia</a> and bore a haemophiliac heir, <a href="/wiki/Alexei_Nikolaevich,_Tsarevich_of_Russia" title="Alexei Nikolaevich, Tsarevich of Russia">Alexei Nikolaevich, Tsarevich of Russia</a>. Her reputation for encouraging her husband's resistance to the surrender of autocratic authority and her known faith in the Russian mystic <a href="/wiki/Grigori_Rasputin" title="Grigori Rasputin">Grigori Rasputin</a> severely damaged her popularity and that of the <a href="/wiki/House_of_Holstein-Gottorp-Romanov" class="mw-redirect" title="House of Holstein-Gottorp-Romanov">Romanov</a> monarchy in its final years.<sup id="cite_ref-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup> She and her immediate family were all <a href="/wiki/Murder_of_the_Romanov_family" title="Murder of the Romanov family">murdered while in Bolshevik captivity in 1918</a>, during the <a href="/wiki/Russian_Revolution" title="Russian Revolution">Russian Revolution</a>. In 2000, the Russian Orthodox Church <a href="/wiki/Glorification" title="Glorification">canonized</a> her as <b>Saint Alexandra the Passion Bearer</b>.
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Appearance_and_personality">Appearance and personality</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Alexandra_Feodorovna_(Alix_of_Hesse)&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Appearance and personality">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Alexandra_Fyodorovna_of_Russia.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5d/Alexandra_Fyodorovna_of_Russia.jpg/220px-Alexandra_Fyodorovna_of_Russia.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="309" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5d/Alexandra_Fyodorovna_of_Russia.jpg/330px-Alexandra_Fyodorovna_of_Russia.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5d/Alexandra_Fyodorovna_of_Russia.jpg/440px-Alexandra_Fyodorovna_of_Russia.jpg 2x" data-file-width="540" data-file-height="758" /></a><figcaption>Alexandra, 1895</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Alexandra_Fyodorovna_1900.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/30/Alexandra_Fyodorovna_1900.jpg/220px-Alexandra_Fyodorovna_1900.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="350" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/30/Alexandra_Fyodorovna_1900.jpg/330px-Alexandra_Fyodorovna_1900.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/30/Alexandra_Fyodorovna_1900.jpg/440px-Alexandra_Fyodorovna_1900.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1939" data-file-height="3085" /></a><figcaption>Alexandra, <abbr title="circa">c.</abbr><span style="white-space:nowrap;">&#8201;1900</span></figcaption></figure>
<p>Alexandra was a noted beauty. Her maternal grandmother <a href="/wiki/Queen_Victoria" title="Queen Victoria">Queen Victoria</a> praised her as "a most lovely child."<sup id="cite_ref-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup> Her friend <a href="/wiki/Anna_Vyrubova" title="Anna Vyrubova">Anna Vyrubova</a> described her as "tall...and delicately, beautifully shaped, with exquisitely white neck and shoulders. Her abundant hair, red gold, was so long that she could easily sit upon it when it was unbound. Her complexion was clear and as rosy as a little child's. The Empress had large eyes, deep gray and very lustrous."<sup id="cite_ref-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-4">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup> A lady-in-waiting, Baroness <a href="/wiki/Sophie_Buxhoeveden" title="Sophie Buxhoeveden">Sophie Buxhoeveden</a>, said that she was "a tall, slim girl" with "beautiful luminous eyes," "regular features," a "very good complexion," and "beautiful golden hair."<sup id="cite_ref-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> An Imperial courtier commented favourably about "her wonderful hair which lay like a heavy crown on her head and large dark-blue eyes beneath long lashes."<sup id="cite_ref-Iroshnikov_p._123_6-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Iroshnikov_p._123-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> In 1905, her daughters' tutor <a href="/wiki/Pierre_Gilliard" title="Pierre Gilliard">Pierre Gilliard</a> wrote that "the Tsarina was still a beautiful woman at that time. She was tall and slender and carried herself superbly. But all this ceased the moment one looked into her eyes—those speaking, grey-blue eyes which mirrored the emotions of a sensitive soul."<sup id="cite_ref-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-7">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Alexandra was shy. When her grandmother Queen Victoria insisted that she play the piano for others, she felt that her "clammy hands... [were] literally glued to the keys" and later described the experience as "one of the worst ordeals" of her life.<sup id="cite_ref-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> When she was Empress, a page in the Imperial household described her as "so obviously nervous of conversation" and claimed that "at moments when she needed to show some social graces or a charming smile, her face would become suffused with little red spots and she would look intensely serious."<sup id="cite_ref-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Grand_Duke_Konstantin_Konstantinovich_of_Russia" title="Grand Duke Konstantin Konstantinovich of Russia">Grand Duke Constantine Constantinovich of Russia</a> noted that she "is terribly shy... It's noticeable that she does not have her mother-in-law's charm, and still does not, therefore, inspire general adulation."<sup id="cite_ref-Julia_P._Gelardi_p.170_10-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Julia_P._Gelardi_p.170-10">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> Nadine Wonar-Larsky, her lady-in-waiting, noted that she was "extremely shy even at such an informal affair as receiving" Wonlar-Larsky and her mother to tea.<sup id="cite_ref-Julia_P._Gelardi_p.170_10-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Julia_P._Gelardi_p.170-10">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> An Imperial courtier notes that "when she was conversing or grew tired, her face became covered in red blotches [and] her hands were red and fleshy."<sup id="cite_ref-Iroshnikov_p._123_6-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Iroshnikov_p._123-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> She herself admitted that during social functions, she "long[ed] to disappear into the ground."<sup id="cite_ref-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-11">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup> She told her friend Marie Bariatinsky that "I am not made to shine before an assembly—I have not got the easy nor the witty talk one needs for that."<sup id="cite_ref-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> This was often mistaken as haughtiness. Her brother <a href="/wiki/Ernest_Louis,_Grand_Duke_of_Hesse" title="Ernest Louis, Grand Duke of Hesse">Ernest Louis</a> reflected that "she would unsmilingly tilt her head to one side if something displeased her, with the result that people often thought that she was unhappy, or bored, or simply capricious."<sup id="cite_ref-13" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> Her daughters' tutor <a href="/wiki/Pierre_Gilliard" title="Pierre Gilliard">Pierre Gilliard</a> reflected that "the reserve which so many people had taken as an affront and had made her so many enemies was rather the effect of a natural timidity, as it were—a mask covering her sensitiveness."<sup id="cite_ref-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-14">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Group_Portrait_of_Royal_Children_1885.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4f/Group_Portrait_of_Royal_Children_1885.jpg/220px-Group_Portrait_of_Royal_Children_1885.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="164" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4f/Group_Portrait_of_Royal_Children_1885.jpg/330px-Group_Portrait_of_Royal_Children_1885.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4f/Group_Portrait_of_Royal_Children_1885.jpg/440px-Group_Portrait_of_Royal_Children_1885.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1668" data-file-height="1244" /></a><figcaption>Left to right (back row): Princess Alix of Hesse; Princess Irene of Hesse; (front row): Princess Marie Louise of Schleswig-Holstein; Charlotte, Princess Bernhard of Saxe-Meiningen; Princess Helena Victoria of Schleswig-Holstein.</figcaption></figure>
<p>Even from a young age, Alexandra was serious and melancholic. Her first cousin and childhood friend <a href="/wiki/Princess_Marie_Louise_of_Schleswig-Holstein" title="Princess Marie Louise of Schleswig-Holstein">Princess Marie Louise</a> said that she had "a curious atmosphere of fatality."<sup id="cite_ref-Almedingen,_The_Empress_Alexandra_1961_p._13_15-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Almedingen,_The_Empress_Alexandra_1961_p._13-15">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> Princess Marie Louise allegedly asked her, "Alix, you always play at being sorrowful; one day the Almighty will send you some real crushing sorrows, and then what are you going to do?"<sup id="cite_ref-Almedingen,_The_Empress_Alexandra_1961_p._13_15-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Almedingen,_The_Empress_Alexandra_1961_p._13-15">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> Sir <a href="/wiki/George_Buchanan_(diplomat)" title="George Buchanan (diplomat)">George William Buchanan</a>, who was a diplomat for Alexandra's grandmother Queen Victoria, reflected that Alexandra had a "sad and pathetic expression."<sup id="cite_ref-16" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Alexandra was devoutly religious. Although she loved <a href="/wiki/Nicholas_II_of_Russia" title="Nicholas II of Russia">Nicholas II</a>, she initially refused his proposal because she refused to convert from <a href="/wiki/Lutheranism" title="Lutheranism">Lutheranism</a> and join the <a href="/wiki/Russian_Orthodox_Church" title="Russian Orthodox Church">Russian Orthodox Church</a>, as was expected of all wives of Russian Emperors. She told Nicholas that even though "it grieves me terribly and makes me very unhappy" not to marry him, leaving the Lutheran church would be "a wrongful thing."<sup id="cite_ref-17" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-17">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup> Generous to her friends, she would try to help others: her lady-in-waiting Sophie Buxhoeveden wrote that she was "ready to do literally anything for her friends" and that "she would take up things and people with violent enthusiasm."<sup id="cite_ref-18" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-18">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> Alexandra admitted that "I am of the preacher type. I want to help others in life, to help them to fight their battles and bear their crosses."<sup id="cite_ref-19" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-19">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup>. Such feelings give a clue to her friendship with the plain, naive <a href="/wiki/Anna_Vyrubova" title="Anna Vyrubova">Anna Vyrubova</a> whom she consoled after a brief and disastrous marriage.
</p><p>Princess Alice had encouraged in her children the importance of and potential learning from literature and Alexandra grew up well-read in English and German. In her first years as Empress, she translated Russian writings and studied Russian music to improve her command of the language. She read <a href="/wiki/Leo_Tolstoy" title="Leo Tolstoy">Leo Tolstoy</a>'s novels and discussed them with her husband.<sup id="cite_ref-20" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-20">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Alexandra enjoyed music. When she was young, she played the banjo and sang duets for hours with Queen Victoria's lady-in-waiting Minnie Cochrane.<sup id="cite_ref-21" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> She enjoyed playing the piano with her daughter <a href="/wiki/Grand_Duchess_Olga_Nikolaevna_of_Russia" title="Grand Duchess Olga Nikolaevna of Russia">Olga</a>, who inherited her musical talent.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_life">Early life</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Alexandra_Feodorovna_(Alix_of_Hesse)&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Early life">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Princess_Alix_of_Hesse_1881.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/76/Princess_Alix_of_Hesse_1881.jpg/220px-Princess_Alix_of_Hesse_1881.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="327" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/76/Princess_Alix_of_Hesse_1881.jpg/330px-Princess_Alix_of_Hesse_1881.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/76/Princess_Alix_of_Hesse_1881.jpg/440px-Princess_Alix_of_Hesse_1881.jpg 2x" data-file-width="848" data-file-height="1260" /></a><figcaption>Princess Alix of Hesse and by Rhine when she was a child</figcaption></figure>
<p>Alexandra was born on 6 June 1872 at the <a href="https://de.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neues_Palais_(Darmstadt)" class="extiw" title="de:Neues Palais (Darmstadt)">New Palace</a> in <a href="/wiki/Darmstadt" title="Darmstadt">Darmstadt</a> as Princess <i>Alix Viktoria Helene Luise Beatrix</i> of <a href="/wiki/Grand_Duchy_of_Hesse" title="Grand Duchy of Hesse">Hesse and by Rhine</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-22" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-22">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-23" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-23">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup> a <a href="/wiki/Grand_Duchy" class="mw-redirect" title="Grand Duchy">Grand Duchy</a> then part of the <a href="/wiki/German_Empire" title="German Empire">German Empire</a>. She was the sixth child and fourth daughter among the seven children of <a href="/wiki/Louis_IV,_Grand_Duke_of_Hesse" title="Louis IV, Grand Duke of Hesse">Louis IV, Grand Duke of Hesse</a>, and his first wife, <a href="/wiki/Princess_Alice_of_the_United_Kingdom" title="Princess Alice of the United Kingdom">Princess Alice of the United Kingdom</a>, the second daughter of <a href="/wiki/Queen_Victoria" title="Queen Victoria">Queen Victoria</a> and her husband <a href="/wiki/Albert,_Prince_Consort" class="mw-redirect" title="Albert, Prince Consort">Albert, Prince Consort</a>.
</p><p>Alix was baptized on 1 July 1872 (her parents' tenth wedding anniversary) in the Protestant <a href="/wiki/Lutheran_Church" class="mw-redirect" title="Lutheran Church">Lutheran Church</a> and given the names of her mother and each of her mother's four sisters, some of which were transliterated into German. Her mother wrote to Queen Victoria, "'Alix' we gave for 'Alice' as they murder my name here: 'Ali-ice' they pronounce it, so we thought 'Alix' could not so easily be spoilt."<sup id="cite_ref-24" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-24">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup> Her mother gave her the nickname of "Sunny", due to her cheerful disposition, a name adopted later by her husband. Her British relatives nicknamed her as "Alicky", to distinguish her from her aunt-by-marriage, <a href="/wiki/Alexandra_of_Denmark" title="Alexandra of Denmark">Alexandra</a>, Princess of Wales, who was known within the family as Alix.<sup id="cite_ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Alix's godparents were the <a href="/wiki/Edward_VII" title="Edward VII">Prince</a> and <a href="/wiki/Alexandra_of_Denmark" title="Alexandra of Denmark">Princess of Wales</a> (her maternal uncle and aunt), <a href="/wiki/Princess_Beatrice_of_the_United_Kingdom" title="Princess Beatrice of the United Kingdom">Princess Beatrice of the United Kingdom</a> (her maternal aunt), the <a href="/wiki/Princess_Augusta_of_Hesse-Kassel" title="Princess Augusta of Hesse-Kassel">Duchess of Cambridge</a> (her great-great-aunt), the <a href="/wiki/Alexander_III_of_Russia" title="Alexander III of Russia">Tsesarevich</a> and <a href="/wiki/Maria_Feodorovna_(Dagmar_of_Denmark)" title="Maria Feodorovna (Dagmar of Denmark)">Tsesarevna</a> of Russia (her future parents-in-law), and <a href="/wiki/Princess_Anna_of_Prussia" title="Princess Anna of Prussia">Princess Anna of Prussia</a>.
</p><p>Alix's older brother <a href="/wiki/Prince_Friedrich_of_Hesse_and_by_Rhine" title="Prince Friedrich of Hesse and by Rhine">Prince Friedrich of Hesse and by Rhine</a> ("Frittie") suffered from <a href="/wiki/Hemophilia" class="mw-redirect" title="Hemophilia">hemophilia</a> and died in May 1873 after a fall, when Alix was about one year old. Of her siblings, Alix was closest to <a href="/wiki/Princess_Marie_of_Hesse_and_by_Rhine_(1874%E2%80%931878)" title="Princess Marie of Hesse and by Rhine (1874–1878)">Princess Marie</a> ("May"), who was two years younger; they were noted as "inseparable".
</p><p>In November 1878, <a href="/wiki/Diphtheria" title="Diphtheria">diphtheria</a> swept through the <a href="/wiki/House_of_Hesse" title="House of Hesse">House of Hesse</a>; Alix, her three sisters, her brother <a href="/wiki/Ernest_Louis,_Grand_Duke_of_Hesse" title="Ernest Louis, Grand Duke of Hesse">Ernst</a> ("Ernie"), and their father fell ill. Elisabeth ("Ella"), Alix's older sister, was visiting their paternal grandmother, and escaped the outbreak. Alix's mother Alice tended to the children herself, rather than abandon them to nurses and doctors. Alice fell ill and died on 14 December 1878, when Alix was six years old. This was the 17th anniversary of <a href="/wiki/Albert,_Prince_Consort" class="mw-redirect" title="Albert, Prince Consort">Alice's own father</a>'s death. Marie also died, but the rest of the siblings survived. She described her childhood before her mother and sister's deaths as "unclouded, happy babyhood, of perpetual sunshine, then of a great cloud".<sup id="cite_ref-26" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-26">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:The_Hessian_children_with_their_grandmother,_Queen_Victoria.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9d/The_Hessian_children_with_their_grandmother%2C_Queen_Victoria.jpg/220px-The_Hessian_children_with_their_grandmother%2C_Queen_Victoria.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="305" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9d/The_Hessian_children_with_their_grandmother%2C_Queen_Victoria.jpg/330px-The_Hessian_children_with_their_grandmother%2C_Queen_Victoria.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/9/9d/The_Hessian_children_with_their_grandmother%2C_Queen_Victoria.jpg 2x" data-file-width="382" data-file-height="530" /></a><figcaption>Princess Alix of Hesse, lower right, with her grandmother Queen Victoria and her four older siblings in mourning after the deaths of her mother and sister. January 1879</figcaption></figure>
<p>Queen Victoria doted on the motherless Alix and became a surrogate mother to Alix. She felt highly protective of Alix and declared that "while I live Alicky, til she is married, will be more than ever my own child."<sup id="cite_ref-27" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-27">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup> She handpicked Alix's tutors and instructed them to send detailed reports back to Windsor every month. She invited Alix and her surviving siblings to England for their holidays, and they grew close to their <a href="/wiki/British_royal_family" title="British royal family">British cousins</a>. Every birthday and Christmas, she sent Alix gifts of dresses, jewelry, lace, and dolls. Unlike her other siblings, Alix signed herself "your loving and grateful child," rather than grandchild, in her letters. Alix reflected that she saw Queen Victoria as "the best and dearest of grandmamas," "a very august person," "a Santa Clause," <sup id="cite_ref-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-28">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup> and "the dearest and kindest Woman alive." When she was betrothed to Nicholas, Alix assured Victoria that "my marrying will [not] make a difference to my love for You."<sup id="cite_ref-29" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> When Queen Victoria died in 1901, Alix openly wept at her memorial service in St. Petersburg and shocked the Russian courtiers who considered her cold and unfeeling.<sup id="cite_ref-30" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-30">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Along with her sister, Princess Irene, Alix was a bridesmaid at the 1885 wedding of her godmother and maternal aunt, Princess Beatrice, to <a href="/wiki/Prince_Henry_of_Battenberg" title="Prince Henry of Battenberg">Prince Henry of Battenberg</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-31" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-31">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup> At the age of 15, she attended Queen Victoria's Golden Jubilee celebrations in 1887.
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Princess_Alix_of_Hesse_1887.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/86/Princess_Alix_of_Hesse_1887.jpg/220px-Princess_Alix_of_Hesse_1887.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="323" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/86/Princess_Alix_of_Hesse_1887.jpg/330px-Princess_Alix_of_Hesse_1887.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/86/Princess_Alix_of_Hesse_1887.jpg/440px-Princess_Alix_of_Hesse_1887.jpg 2x" data-file-width="970" data-file-height="1424" /></a><figcaption>Princess Alix when she was 15</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Hessen_Family_in_mouring.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f0/Hessen_Family_in_mouring.jpg/220px-Hessen_Family_in_mouring.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="335" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f0/Hessen_Family_in_mouring.jpg/330px-Hessen_Family_in_mouring.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f0/Hessen_Family_in_mouring.jpg/440px-Hessen_Family_in_mouring.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1736" data-file-height="2640" /></a><figcaption>Alix of Hesse (center) with her siblings, Ernest Louis, Grand Duke of Hesse, Victoria of Hesse, Irene of Hesse, and Elisabeth Feodorovna (née Princess Elisabeth of Hesse and by Rhine), in mourning for their father's death, 1892</figcaption></figure>
<p>In March 1892, when Alix was nineteen years old, her father Grand Duke Louis IV, died of a heart attack.<sup id="cite_ref-32" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-32">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup> According to her biographer, Baroness Buxhoeveden, Alix regarded the death of her father as the greatest sorrow of her life. Buxhoeveden recalled in her 1928 biography that "for years she could not speak of him, and long after when she was in Russia, anything that reminded her of him would bring her to the verge of tears".
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Proposed_matches">Proposed matches</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Alexandra_Feodorovna_(Alix_of_Hesse)&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Proposed matches">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Queen Victoria greatly favored Alix and she wanted Alix to become the Queen Consort of the United Kingdom, which she considered "the greatest position there is."<sup id="cite_ref-James_Pope-Hennessy_1960_p._183_33-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-James_Pope-Hennessy_1960_p._183-33">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup> On 2 March 1888, she wrote to Alix's oldest sister Victoria that "My heart and mind are bent on securing dear Alicky for either <a href="/wiki/Prince_Albert_Victor,_Duke_of_Clarence_and_Avondale" title="Prince Albert Victor, Duke of Clarence and Avondale">Eddie</a> or <a href="/wiki/George_V" title="George V">Georgie</a>."<sup id="cite_ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup> She pressured Alix to accept a proposal from her first cousin and the <a href="/wiki/Heir_apparent" title="Heir apparent">heir apparent</a> to the British throne, <a href="/wiki/Prince_Albert_Victor,_Duke_of_Clarence_and_Avondale" title="Prince Albert Victor, Duke of Clarence and Avondale">Prince Albert Victor, Duke of Clarence and Avondale</a>. In 1889, Victoria invited Alix and Eddy to Balmoral in hopes that they would fall in love. Eddy grew infatuated with her and proposed, but Alix was not interested in him and rejected his proposal. However, Victoria still persisted and tried to convince Alix of the benefits of the match. Victoria wrote to <a href="/wiki/Princess_Victoria_of_Hesse_and_by_Rhine" title="Princess Victoria of Hesse and by Rhine">Princess Victoria of Hesse and by Rhine</a>, Alix's older sister, that Alix "should be made to reflect seriously on the folly of throwing away the chance of a very good husband, kind, affectionate and steady, and of entering a united happy family and a very good position which is second to none in the world!"<sup id="cite_ref-35" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-35">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup> Alix's older sister Ella opposed the match because "he [Eddy] does not look over strong and is too stupid."<sup id="cite_ref-36" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-36">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup> In May 1890, Alix wrote a letter to Eddy that although it "pained her to pain him,"<sup id="cite_ref-James_Pope-Hennessy_1960_p._183_33-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-James_Pope-Hennessy_1960_p._183-33">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup> she only saw him as a cousin and could not marry him. She wrote to Victoria that she would marry Eddy if she were "forced" by the family but that both of them would be miserable. Victoria was disappointed, but she decided that Alix had shown "great strength of character" in refusing to acquiesce to such strong pressure.<sup id="cite_ref-James_Pope-Hennessy_1960_p._183_33-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-James_Pope-Hennessy_1960_p._183-33">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1891, Queen Victoria tried to arrange a match between Alix and <a href="/wiki/Prince_Maximilian_of_Baden" title="Prince Maximilian of Baden">Prince Maximilian of Baden</a>. She asked Louis to invite Max to Darmstadt as soon as possible. When he arrived in Darmstadt, Max told Alix that he intended to propose to her. Alix was surprised and unhappy, and she later reflected that "I did not know him at all."<sup id="cite_ref-37" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-37">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup> She asked her older sister Victoria to intervene and help her reject Max politely.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Engagement">Engagement</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Alexandra_Feodorovna_(Alix_of_Hesse)&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Engagement">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Princess_Alix_of_Hesse_1890.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d4/Princess_Alix_of_Hesse_1890.jpg/220px-Princess_Alix_of_Hesse_1890.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="334" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d4/Princess_Alix_of_Hesse_1890.jpg/330px-Princess_Alix_of_Hesse_1890.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d4/Princess_Alix_of_Hesse_1890.jpg/440px-Princess_Alix_of_Hesse_1890.jpg 2x" data-file-width="926" data-file-height="1404" /></a><figcaption>Alix of Hesse, 1890</figcaption></figure>

<a href="/wiki/Louis_IV,_Grand_Duke_of_Hesse" title="Louis IV, Grand Duke of Hesse">Louis IV</a></th></tr><tr><td class="sidebar-content" style="padding-left: 5px; padding-right: 5px; text-align: left;">
<dl><dt>Children</dt>
<dd><a href="/wiki/Princess_Victoria_of_Hesse_and_by_Rhine" title="Princess Victoria of Hesse and by Rhine">Victoria, Marchioness of Milford Haven</a></dd>
<dd><a href="/wiki/Princess_Elisabeth_of_Hesse_and_by_Rhine_(1864%E2%80%931918)" title="Princess Elisabeth of Hesse and by Rhine (1864–1918)">Grand Duchess Elizaveta Feodorovna of Russia</a></dd>
<dd><a href="/wiki/Princess_Irene_of_Hesse_and_by_Rhine" title="Princess Irene of Hesse and by Rhine">Irene, Princess Heinrich of Prussia</a></dd>
<dd><a href="/wiki/Ernest_Louis,_Grand_Duke_of_Hesse" title="Ernest Louis, Grand Duke of Hesse">Ernest Louis</a></dd>
<dd><a href="/wiki/Prince_Friedrich_of_Hesse_and_by_Rhine" title="Prince Friedrich of Hesse and by Rhine">Prince Friedrich</a></dd>
<dd><a class="mw-selflink selflink">Alexandra Feodorovna, Empress of Russia</a></dd>
<dd><a href="/wiki/Princess_Marie_of_Hesse_and_by_Rhine_(1874%E2%80%931878)" title="Princess Marie of Hesse and by Rhine (1874–1878)">Princess Marie</a></dd></dl></td>
</tr><tr><td class="sidebar-navbar" style="padding-right: 5px;"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1129693374"><style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1063604349">.mw-parser-output .navbar{display:inline;font-size:88%;font-weight:normal}.mw-parser-output .navbar-collapse{float:left;text-align:left}.mw-parser-output .navbar-boxtext{word-spacing:0}.mw-parser-output .navbar ul{display:inline-block;white-space:nowrap;line-height:inherit}.mw-parser-output .navbar-brackets::before{margin-right:-0.125em;content:"[ "}.mw-parser-output .navbar-brackets::after{margin-left:-0.125em;content:" ]"}.mw-parser-output .navbar li{word-spacing:-0.125em}.mw-parser-output .navbar a>span,.mw-parser-output .navbar a>abbr{text-decoration:inherit}.mw-parser-output .navbar-mini abbr{font-variant:small-caps;border-bottom:none;text-decoration:none;cursor:inherit}.mw-parser-output .navbar-ct-full{font-size:114%;margin:0 7em}.mw-parser-output .navbar-ct-mini{font-size:114%;margin:0 4em}</style><div class="navbar plainlinks hlist navbar-mini"><ul><li class="nv-view"><a href="/wiki/Template:House_of_Hesse_and_by_Rhine" title="Template:House of Hesse and by Rhine"><abbr title="View this template">v</abbr></a></li><li class="nv-talk"><a href="/wiki/Template_talk:House_of_Hesse_and_by_Rhine" title="Template talk:House of Hesse and by Rhine"><abbr title="Discuss this template">t</abbr></a></li><li class="nv-edit"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Template:House_of_Hesse_and_by_Rhine&amp;action=edit"><abbr title="Edit this template">e</abbr></a></li></ul></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>In 1884, Alix attended the wedding of her sister <a href="/wiki/Princess_Elisabeth_of_Hesse_and_by_Rhine_(1864%E2%80%931918)" title="Princess Elisabeth of Hesse and by Rhine (1864–1918)">Elisabeth</a> to <a href="/wiki/Grand_Duke_Sergei_Alexandrovich_of_Russia" title="Grand Duke Sergei Alexandrovich of Russia">Grand Duke Sergei Alexandrovich</a> in St. Petersburg. At this wedding, the 12-year-old Alix met the 16-year-old <a href="/wiki/Nicholas_II_of_Russia" title="Nicholas II of Russia">Grand Duke Nicholas</a>, nephew of the groom and heir-apparent to the Imperial throne of Russia. In his diary Nicholas called Alix "sweet little Alix"<sup id="cite_ref-38" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-38">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup> and declared "we love each other." He gave her a brooch as a sign of his affection, and they scratched their names into a windowpane.
</p><p>In January 1890, Alix visited her sister Ella in Russia. She and Nicholas skated together, met at tea parties, and played badminton. Nicholas wrote in his diary: "It is my dream to one day marry Alix H. I have loved her for a long time, but more deeply and strongly since 1889 when she spent six weeks in Petersburg. For a long time, I have resisted my feeling that my dearest dream will come true."<sup id="cite_ref-Massie,_R_p.49_39-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Massie,_R_p.49-39">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Alix's sister Ella and her husband Sergei were enthusiastically in favor of the match between Nicholas and Alix. The future Edward VII told his mother Queen Victoria that "Ella will move heaven and earth to get [Alix] to marry a Grand Duke."<sup id="cite_ref-40" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-40">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup> Ella wrote to Ernest, "God grant this marriage will come true."<sup id="cite_ref-41" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-41">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Nicholas and Alix were second cousins through a great-grandmother, <a href="/wiki/Princess_Wilhelmine_of_Baden" title="Princess Wilhelmine of Baden">Princess Wilhelmina of Baden</a>, and they were third cousins once removed through <a href="/wiki/Frederick_William_II_of_Prussia" title="Frederick William II of Prussia">Frederick William II of Prussia</a>, who was Alix's great-great-grandfather and Nicholas's great-great-great-grandfather. Nicholas's mother, Empress <a href="/wiki/Maria_Feodorovna_(Dagmar_of_Denmark)" title="Maria Feodorovna (Dagmar of Denmark)">Maria Feodorovna (Dagmar of Denmark)</a>, was Alix's godmother and the younger sister of <a href="/wiki/Alexandra_of_Denmark" title="Alexandra of Denmark">Alexandra of Denmark</a>, who married Alix's uncle <a href="/wiki/Edward_VII" title="Edward VII">Edward VII</a>. Her sister Ella had married Nicholas's uncle Sergei. Her uncle <a href="/wiki/Alfred,_Duke_of_Saxe-Coburg_and_Gotha" title="Alfred, Duke of Saxe-Coburg and Gotha">Prince Alfred, Duke of Edinburgh</a>, had married Nicholas's aunt <a href="/wiki/Grand_Duchess_Maria_Alexandrovna_of_Russia" title="Grand Duchess Maria Alexandrovna of Russia">Grand Duchess Maria Alexandrovna of Russia</a>.
</p><p>Queen Victoria opposed the match to Nicholas. She personally liked Nicholas, but she disliked Russia and Nicholas's father and worried that Alix would not be safe in Russia. She wrote to Alix's older sister <a href="/wiki/Princess_Victoria_of_Hesse_and_by_Rhine" title="Princess Victoria of Hesse and by Rhine">Victoria</a> of her suspicions that Sergei and Ella were encouraging the match.<sup id="cite_ref-42" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-42">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup> After the betrothal was announced, she reflected: "The more I think of sweet Alicky's marriage the more unhappy I am. Not as to the personality for I like [Nicholas] very much but on account of the country and the awful insecurity to which that poor child will be exposed."<sup id="cite_ref-43" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-43">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Alexander and Maria Feodorovna were both vehemently <a href="/wiki/Anti-German_sentiment" title="Anti-German sentiment">anti-German</a> and did not want Alix as a daughter-in-law. Maria Feodorovna told her sister <a href="/wiki/Alexandra_of_Denmark" title="Alexandra of Denmark">Alexandra of Denmark</a> that the youngest daughter of an undistinguished grand duke was not worthy to marry the heir to the Russian throne, and she believed that Alix was too tactless and unlikeable to be a successful Empress.<sup id="cite_ref-44" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-44">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup> Alexander favored <a href="/wiki/Princess_H%C3%A9l%C3%A8ne_of_Orl%C3%A9ans" title="Princess Hélène of Orléans">Princess Hélène</a>, the tall, dark-haired daughter of <a href="/wiki/Philippe,_Comte_de_Paris" class="mw-redirect" title="Philippe, Comte de Paris">Philippe, Comte de Paris</a>, pretender to the throne of France.<sup id="cite_ref-Massie,_R_p.49_39-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Massie,_R_p.49-39">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup> Nicholas was not attracted to Hélène, writing in his diary: "Mama made a few allusions to Hélène, daughter of the Comte de Paris. I myself want to go in one direction and it is evident that Mama wants me to choose the other one."<sup id="cite_ref-45" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-45">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup> Hélène also resisted this match, as she was <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic" class="mw-redirect" title="Roman Catholic">Roman Catholic</a> and her father refused to allow her to convert to <a href="/wiki/Russian_Orthodoxy" title="Russian Orthodoxy">Russian Orthodoxy</a>. Alexander sent emissaries to <a href="/wiki/Princess_Margaret_of_Prussia" title="Princess Margaret of Prussia">Princess Margaret of Prussia</a>, sister of <a href="/wiki/Wilhelm_II,_German_Emperor" title="Wilhelm II, German Emperor">German Emperor Wilhelm II</a>, and a granddaughter of Queen Victoria. Nicholas declared that he would rather become a monk than marry Margaret; she in turn was unwilling to convert to the Russian Orthodox Church from being Protestant.
</p><p>When his health failed in 1894, Alexander III decided to allow Nicholas to marry Alix so that he could secure the succession.<sup id="cite_ref-46" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-46">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup> Marie reluctantly permitted Nicholas to propose to Alix. Nicholas was ecstatic and immediately inquired about Alix.
</p><p>Despite her love for Nicholas, Alix was initially reluctant to marry Nicholas because she did not want to renounce her Lutheran faith to join the Orthodox church. She wrote to Nicholas that "I cannot [convert to Orthodoxy] against my conscience" because "What happiness can come from a marriage which begins without the real blessing of God?"<sup id="cite_ref-47" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-47">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup> Nicholas was devastated, but he remained hopeful because Ella assured him that Alix was "utterly miserable" and had a "deep and pure" love for him.<sup id="cite_ref-Lifelong_Passion,_p._34_48-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Lifelong_Passion,_p._34-48">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup> Nicholas begged her "not [to] say 'no' directly" and declared, "Do you think there can exist any happiness in the whole world without you!"<sup id="cite_ref-Lifelong_Passion,_p._34_48-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Lifelong_Passion,_p._34-48">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In April 1894, Alix's brother <a href="/wiki/Ernest_Louis,_Grand_Duke_of_Hesse" title="Ernest Louis, Grand Duke of Hesse">Ernest Louis</a> married <a href="/wiki/Princess_Victoria_Melita_of_Saxe-Coburg_and_Gotha" title="Princess Victoria Melita of Saxe-Coburg and Gotha">Princess Victoria Melita of Saxe-Coburg and Gotha</a>. Princess Victoria was Alexander III's niece by his sister <a href="/wiki/Grand_Duchess_Maria_Alexandrovna_of_Russia" title="Grand Duchess Maria Alexandrovna of Russia">Grand Duchess Maria Alexandrovna of Russia</a> and Nicholas's first cousin, so several Russians attended the wedding, including Grand Dukes Vladimir, Sergei and Paul, Grand Duchesses Elisabeth Feodorovna and Maria Pavlovna, and Nicholas.<sup id="cite_ref-49" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-49">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup> Nicholas was determined to convince Alix to marry him. He was evidently confident in his future success: He brought Father Ioann Yanyshev, confessor to the Imperial family, to teach Alix about Russian Orthodoxy, and he brought Ekaterina Adolfovna Schneider, to teach Alix Russian.<sup id="cite_ref-50" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-50">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The day after his arrival in Coburg, Nicholas proposed to Alix and tried for two hours to convince her to convert to Orthodoxy. She wept continuously but refused. Ella spoke to Alix afterwards, and she convinced Alix that she did not need to renounce Lutheranism to convert to Orthodoxy. Ella herself had not been required to abjure her Lutheran faith when she converted to Orthodoxy. The next day, Alix spoke to Wilhelm II (who hoped that a German Empress would lead to better German-Russian relations) and <a href="/wiki/Duchess_Marie_of_Mecklenburg-Schwerin" title="Duchess Marie of Mecklenburg-Schwerin">Duchess Marie of Mecklenburg-Schwerin</a> (a German princess who had converted from Lutheranism to Orthodoxy to marry Nicholas's uncle <a href="/wiki/Grand_Duke_Vladimir_Alexandrovich_of_Russia" title="Grand Duke Vladimir Alexandrovich of Russia">Grand Duke Vladimir Alexandrovich of Russia</a>). She accepted Nicholas's second proposal.<sup id="cite_ref-51" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-51">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Engagement_official_picture_of_Alexandra_and_Nicholas.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/95/Engagement_official_picture_of_Alexandra_and_Nicholas.jpg/180px-Engagement_official_picture_of_Alexandra_and_Nicholas.jpg" decoding="async" width="180" height="245" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/95/Engagement_official_picture_of_Alexandra_and_Nicholas.jpg/270px-Engagement_official_picture_of_Alexandra_and_Nicholas.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/95/Engagement_official_picture_of_Alexandra_and_Nicholas.jpg/360px-Engagement_official_picture_of_Alexandra_and_Nicholas.jpg 2x" data-file-width="716" data-file-height="976" /></a><figcaption>Tsar Nicholas II, in hussar uniform, and Princess Alix of Hesse in an official engagement photograph, 1894</figcaption></figure>
<p>Following the engagement, Alix returned to England and her grandmother. In June, Nicholas travelled to England to visit her and attend the christening of the eldest son of <a href="/wiki/George_V" title="George V">Prince George, Duke of York</a>. Alix and Nicholas were both named as godparents of the boy, who reigned briefly as <a href="/wiki/Edward_VIII" title="Edward VIII">Edward VIII</a> of Great Britain in 1936.<sup id="cite_ref-52" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-52">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup> Alix wrote to her old governess that "I am more happy than words can express. At last, after these five sad years!"<sup id="cite_ref-53" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-53">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup> Nicholas declared that "my soul was brimming with joy and life."<sup id="cite_ref-54" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-54">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In September, as Alexander III's health declined, Nicholas obtained the permission of his dying father to summon Alix to the Romanovs' <a href="/wiki/Crimea" title="Crimea">Crimean</a> palace of <a href="/wiki/Livadia_Palace" title="Livadia Palace">Livadia</a>. Escorted by her sister Ella from <a href="/wiki/Warsaw" title="Warsaw">Warsaw</a> to the Crimea, she traveled by ordinary passenger train.<sup id="cite_ref-55" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-55">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup> The dying tsar insisted on receiving Alix in full dress uniform and gave her his blessing.<sup id="cite_ref-56" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-56">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Empress_of_Russia">Empress of Russia</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Alexandra_Feodorovna_(Alix_of_Hesse)&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Empress of Russia">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Wedding">Wedding</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Alexandra_Feodorovna_(Alix_of_Hesse)&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Wedding">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Wedding_of_Nicholas_II_and_Alexandra_Feodorovna" title="Wedding of Nicholas II and Alexandra Feodorovna">Wedding of Nicholas II and Alexandra Feodorovna</a></div>
<figure typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Wedding_of_Nicholas_II_and_Alexandra_Feodorovna_by_Laurits_Tuxen_(1895-6,_Royal_coll.).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fd/Wedding_of_Nicholas_II_and_Alexandra_Feodorovna_by_Laurits_Tuxen_%281895-6%2C_Royal_coll.%29.jpg/250px-Wedding_of_Nicholas_II_and_Alexandra_Feodorovna_by_Laurits_Tuxen_%281895-6%2C_Royal_coll.%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="250" height="188" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fd/Wedding_of_Nicholas_II_and_Alexandra_Feodorovna_by_Laurits_Tuxen_%281895-6%2C_Royal_coll.%29.jpg/375px-Wedding_of_Nicholas_II_and_Alexandra_Feodorovna_by_Laurits_Tuxen_%281895-6%2C_Royal_coll.%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fd/Wedding_of_Nicholas_II_and_Alexandra_Feodorovna_by_Laurits_Tuxen_%281895-6%2C_Royal_coll.%29.jpg/500px-Wedding_of_Nicholas_II_and_Alexandra_Feodorovna_by_Laurits_Tuxen_%281895-6%2C_Royal_coll.%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1920" data-file-height="1446" /></a><figcaption>Portrait by <a href="/wiki/Laurits_Tuxen" title="Laurits Tuxen">Laurits Tuxen</a> of the wedding of <a href="/wiki/Nicholas_II_of_Russia" title="Nicholas II of Russia">Tsar Nicholas II</a> and the Princess Alix of Hesse-Darmstadt, which took place at the Chapel of the Winter Palace, St Petersburg, on 14/26 November 1894.<sup id="cite_ref-57" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-57">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup></figcaption></figure>
<p>On 1 November 1894, <a href="/wiki/Alexander_III_of_Russia" title="Alexander III of Russia">Alexander III</a> died at the age of 49. Nicholas was confirmed as Tsar Nicholas II. The next day, Alix was received into the Russian Orthodox Church as "the truly believing Grand Duchess Alexandra Feodorovna."<sup id="cite_ref-58" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-58">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup> However, she was not required to repudiate Lutheranism.<sup id="cite_ref-59" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-59">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup> Alix wanted to take the name <i>Yekaterina</i>, but Nicholas wanted her to take the name Alexandra so that they could be a second Nicholas and Alexandra. He was inspired by his great-grandfather <a href="/wiki/Nicholas_I_of_Russia" title="Nicholas I of Russia">Nicholas I</a> and his great-grandmother <a href="/wiki/Alexandra_Feodorovna_(Charlotte_of_Prussia)" title="Alexandra Feodorovna (Charlotte of Prussia)">Alexandra Feodorovna</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-60" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-60">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Alexandra, the <a href="/wiki/Edward_VII" title="Edward VII">Prince</a> and <a href="/wiki/Alexandra_of_Denmark" title="Alexandra of Denmark">Princess of Wales</a>, and Nicholas's Greek relatives accompanied the coffin of Alexander III first through Moscow and St. Petersburg. The funeral of Alexander III occurred on 19 November.
</p><p>On 26 November 1894, Alexandra and Nicholas married in the <a href="/wiki/Grand_Church_of_the_Winter_Palace" title="Grand Church of the Winter Palace">Grand Church of the Winter Palace</a> of <a href="/wiki/Saint_Petersburg" title="Saint Petersburg">Saint Petersburg</a>. Court mourning could be relaxed because it was the birthday of Nicholas's mother, now <a href="/wiki/Maria_Feodorovna_(Dagmar_of_Denmark)" title="Maria Feodorovna (Dagmar of Denmark)">Dowager Empress Maria Feodorovna</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-61" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-61">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup> Many Russians considered Alexandra a bad <a href="/wiki/Omen" title="Omen">omen</a> because she arrived so soon after the death of Emperor Alexander: "She has come to us behind a coffin. She brings misfortune with her."<sup id="cite_ref-62" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-62">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup> Alexandra herself wrote to her sister: "Our wedding seemed to me, a mere continuation of the funeral liturgy for the dead Tsar, with one difference; I wore a white dress instead of a black one."<sup id="cite_ref-63" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-63">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Coronation">Coronation</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Alexandra_Feodorovna_(Alix_of_Hesse)&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Coronation">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Coronation_of_Nicholas_II_and_Alexandra_Feodorovna" title="Coronation of Nicholas II and Alexandra Feodorovna">Coronation of Nicholas II and Alexandra Feodorovna</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Lesser_CoA_of_the_empress_Alexandra_Feodorovna_of_Russia.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a9/Lesser_CoA_of_the_empress_Alexandra_Feodorovna_of_Russia.svg/220px-Lesser_CoA_of_the_empress_Alexandra_Feodorovna_of_Russia.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="285" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a9/Lesser_CoA_of_the_empress_Alexandra_Feodorovna_of_Russia.svg/330px-Lesser_CoA_of_the_empress_Alexandra_Feodorovna_of_Russia.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a9/Lesser_CoA_of_the_empress_Alexandra_Feodorovna_of_Russia.svg/440px-Lesser_CoA_of_the_empress_Alexandra_Feodorovna_of_Russia.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="781" data-file-height="1013" /></a><figcaption>Lesser arms of Empress Alexandra Feodorovna</figcaption></figure>
<p>On 14 May 1896, Alexandra and Nicholas were crowned at the <a href="/wiki/Dormition_Cathedral,_Moscow" title="Dormition Cathedral, Moscow">Dormition Cathedral</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Moscow_Kremlin" class="mw-redirect" title="Moscow Kremlin">Kremlin</a>.
</p><p>Five hundred thousand Russians gathered in the capital to watch the entertainment, eat the court-sponsored food, and collect the gifts in honor of their new tsar. There were rumors that there was not enough food for everyone, so the crowd rushed towards the gift tables. The police failed to maintain order, and a thousand Russians were <a href="/wiki/Khodynka_Tragedy" title="Khodynka Tragedy">trampled to death</a> at the <a href="/wiki/Khodynka_Field" title="Khodynka Field">Khodynka Field</a>.
</p><p>Nicholas and Alexandra were horrified by the deaths, and they decided not to attend the ball that the French ambassador, the Marquis de Montebello, hosted in their honor. Nicholas's uncles urged him to attend so as not to offend the French and give credence to the rumors that the German Alexandra was prejudiced against the French. <a href="/wiki/Sergei_Witte" title="Sergei Witte">Sergei Witte</a> commented, "We expected the party would be called off. Instead it took place as if nothing had happened and the ball was opened by Their Majesties dancing a quadrille."<sup id="cite_ref-Massie,_R_p.80_64-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Massie,_R_p.80-64">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup> The British ambassador informed Queen Victoria that "the Empress appeared in great distress, her eyes reddened by tears."
</p><p>The next day, Alexandra and Nicholas visited the wounded and paid for the coffins of the dead. However, many Russians took the disaster at Khodynka Meadow as an omen that Nicholas's reign would be unhappy. Others used the circumstances of the tragedy and the behaviour of the royal establishment to underscore the heartlessness of the autocracy and the contemptible shallowness of the young tsar and his "German woman".<sup id="cite_ref-65" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-65">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Rejection_by_the_Russian_people">Rejection by the Russian people</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Alexandra_Feodorovna_(Alix_of_Hesse)&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Rejection by the Russian people">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Empress_Alexandra_Feodorovna_of_Russia_(Alix_of_Hesse).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/17/Empress_Alexandra_Feodorovna_of_Russia_%28Alix_of_Hesse%29.jpg/220px-Empress_Alexandra_Feodorovna_of_Russia_%28Alix_of_Hesse%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="288" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/17/Empress_Alexandra_Feodorovna_of_Russia_%28Alix_of_Hesse%29.jpg/330px-Empress_Alexandra_Feodorovna_of_Russia_%28Alix_of_Hesse%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/17/Empress_Alexandra_Feodorovna_of_Russia_%28Alix_of_Hesse%29.jpg/440px-Empress_Alexandra_Feodorovna_of_Russia_%28Alix_of_Hesse%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1208" data-file-height="1584" /></a><figcaption>Alexandra Feodorovna, 1895</figcaption></figure>
<p>Alexandra was extremely unpopular among her husband's Russian subjects. Her shy and introverted nature was interpreted as arrogance and coldness, and she struggled to win friends. The Russian court judged her as "devoid of charm, wooden, cold eyes, holds herself as if she'd swallowed a yardstick."<sup id="cite_ref-66" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-66">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Alexandra struggled to communicate. She spoke English and German fluently, but she struggled to speak French, the official language of the court, and she did not start to learn Russian until she became Empress. She eventually learned Russian, but she spoke haltingly with a strong accent.
</p><p>Alexandra failed to understand her public role at court as the Empress. Traditionally, the Empress led the social scene and hosted numerous balls. However, Alexandra was shocked by the love affairs and gossip that characterized parties. She declared that "the heads of the young ladies of St. Petersburg are filled with nothing but thoughts of young officers,"<sup id="cite_ref-67" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-67">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup> and she crossed off the names of noblemen and noblewomen whom she deemed scandalous from the invitation lists until no one was left. Many people in St. Petersburg society dismissed Alexandra as a prude. In one of her first balls, Alexandra sent a lady-in-waiting to reprimand a young woman in a low-cut gown: "Her Majesty wants me to tell you that in Hesse-Darmstadt we don't wear our dresses this way." The unnamed woman replied, "Pray tell Her Majesty that in Russia we do wear our dresses this way."<sup id="cite_ref-68" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-68">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup> In 1896, she launched the "Help Through Handwork" project. She wanted to create a series of workshops in which noblewomen would teach poor peasants how to sew and raise funds for needy families.<sup id="cite_ref-69" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-69">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Alexandra had a difficult relationship with her mother-in-law, Marie Feodorovna. Unlike other European courts of the day, Russian protocol gave the Dowager Empress seniority in rank to the Empress. At royal balls, Marie entered on her son's arm and Alexandra followed on the arm of one of the grand dukes. Marie was so accustomed to the tradition that she was surprised when Alexandra was bitter about her junior role at court. The crown jewels were the property of the current Empress, but Marie refused to relinquish them to Alexandra. Marie begrudgingly surrendered the magnificent collection when Alexandra threatened to not wear jewels to official court events.
</p><p>Alexandra was unpopular in the Imperial family. She was a fervent advocate of the "<a href="/wiki/Divine_right_of_kings" title="Divine right of kings">divine right of kings</a>" and believed that it was unnecessary to attempt to secure the approval of the people, according to her aunt, <a href="/wiki/Victoria,_Princess_Royal" title="Victoria, Princess Royal">German Empress Victoria</a>, who wrote to Queen Victoria that "Alix is very imperious and will always insist on having her own way; she will never yield one iota of power she will imagine she wields<span class="nowrap">&#160;</span>..."<sup id="cite_ref-King,_G,_The_Last_Empress,_p.93_70-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-King,_G,_The_Last_Empress,_p.93-70">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup> She dreaded social functions and enjoyed being alone with Nicholas, so she did not host the balls and parties that a tsarina normally would. Members of the Imperial family resented that she closed off their access to the tsar and the inner court. She disliked Nicholas's uncle, <a href="/wiki/Grand_Duke_Vladimir_Alexandrovich_of_Russia" title="Grand Duke Vladimir Alexandrovich of Russia">Grand Duke Vladimir Alexandrovich</a>. She declared that Vladimir's sons <a href="/wiki/Grand_Duke_Kirill_Vladimirovich_of_Russia" title="Grand Duke Kirill Vladimirovich of Russia">Kirill</a>, <a href="/wiki/Grand_Duke_Boris_Vladimirovich_of_Russia" title="Grand Duke Boris Vladimirovich of Russia">Boris</a> and <a href="/wiki/Grand_Duke_Andrei_Vladimirovich_of_Russia" title="Grand Duke Andrei Vladimirovich of Russia">Andrei</a> were irredeemably immoral. In 1913, she refused Boris's proposal for the hand of Grand Duchess Olga. During the war, Vladimir's wife, <a href="/wiki/Duchess_Marie_of_Mecklenburg-Schwerin" title="Duchess Marie of Mecklenburg-Schwerin">Grand Duchess Marie Pavlovna</a> openly criticized Alexandra.
</p><p>Insecure about her modest origins as a minor German princess, Alexandra insisted on being treated with the full honors due to an Empress. In 1896, Alexandra and Nicholas went on a European tour. When Wilhelm II lent her an antique silver toilette service that had once belonged to <a href="/wiki/Louise_of_Mecklenburg-Strelitz" title="Louise of Mecklenburg-Strelitz">Queen Louise of Prussia</a>, she was insulted and declared that only a gold service was suitable for an empress. She dressed herself "with great magnificence".<sup id="cite_ref-71" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-71">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup> At the Russian court, courtiers mocked for her "dress[ing] in the heavy brocade of which she was so fond, and with diamonds scattered all over her, in defiance of good taste and common sense."<sup id="cite_ref-72" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-72">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Alexandra refused to court the public because she believed that the Russian people automatically loved and revered their Emperor and Empress. When she and Nicholas were traveling to Crimea by train, hundreds of peasants wore their best clothes and waited overnight to see the Imperial couple. Nicholas went to the window and waved, but Alexandra refused to open the curtains and acknowledge the crowd. Dowager Empress Marie was furious that "[Alexandra] thinks the Imperial family should be 'above that sort of thing.' What does she mean? Above winning the people's affection?...And yet, how often she complains of the public indifference toward her."<sup id="cite_ref-73" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-73">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup> Queen Victoria worried about Alexandra's unpopularity in her new country, and she advised her granddaughter: "I've ruled more than 50 years ... and nevertheless every day I think about what I need to do to retain and strengthen the love of my subjects ... It is your first duty to win their love and respect." Alexandra replied, "You are mistaken, my dear grandmamma; Russia is not England. Here we do not need to earn the love of the people. The Russian people revere their Tsars as divine beings ... As far as Petersburg society is concerned, that is something which one may wholly disregard."<sup id="cite_ref-74" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-74">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Struggle_to_bear_an_heir">Struggle to bear an heir</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Alexandra_Feodorovna_(Alix_of_Hesse)&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Struggle to bear an heir">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>On 15 November 1895, Alexandra gave birth to her eldest child and daughter, <a href="/wiki/Grand_Duchess_Olga_Nikolaevna_of_Russia" title="Grand Duchess Olga Nikolaevna of Russia">Olga</a>, at the Alexander Palace. Many Russians and members of the Imperial family were disappointed in the sex of the child, but Nicholas and Alexandra were delighted with their daughter and doted on her. The birth of Olga did not change Grand Duke George's position as Nicholas's heir presumptive. The Pauline Laws implemented by Tsar Paul I forbade women from taking the Romanov throne as long as any male Romanov was alive. If Alexandra did not bear a son, Nicholas's heirs would be his brothers and uncles. However, few worried because Alexandra was only 23, so she was expected to be able to bear a son soon.
</p><p>A few months after giving birth to Olga, Alexandra was pregnant again. Due to the stress of the coronation, she had a <a href="/wiki/Miscarriage" title="Miscarriage">miscarriage</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-75" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-75">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup> No announcement was made, because she had not publicly confirmed her pregnancy yet. However, there were unfounded and malicious rumors in St. Petersburg that Alexandra had become pregnant by a lover and aborted the baby to hide her infidelity.<sup id="cite_ref-76" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-76">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 10 June 1897, Alexandra gave birth to her second child and daughter, <a href="/wiki/Grand_Duchess_Tatiana_Nikolaevna_of_Russia" title="Grand Duchess Tatiana Nikolaevna of Russia">Tatiana</a>. Nicholas was overjoyed, but the members of his family were unhappy and worried. When she woke up from the chloroform, Alexandra  saw the "anxious and troubled faces" around her and "burst into loud hysterics." She cried, "My God, it is again a daughter. What will the nation say, what will the nation say?"<sup id="cite_ref-77" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-77">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup> Alexandra's inability to have a son made her even more unpopular among the Russians. Nicholas's brother George said that he was disappointed not to have a nephew to relieve him of his duties as heir: "I was already preparing to go into retirement, but it was not to be.<sup id="cite_ref-78" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-78">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 26 June 1899, Alexandra gave birth to her third child and daughter, <a href="/wiki/Grand_Duchess_Maria_Nikolaevna_of_Russia" title="Grand Duchess Maria Nikolaevna of Russia">Maria</a>. Queen Victoria sent Alexandra a telegram when Maria was born: "I am so thankful that dear Alicky has recovered so well, but I regret the third girl for the country."<sup id="cite_ref-79" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-79">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup> Grand Duke Konstantin fretted: "And so there's no Heir. The whole of Russia will be disappointed by this news."<sup id="cite_ref-80" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-80">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup> Russians saw the birth of a third daughter as proof that Alexandra was bad luck. Two weeks after Maria's birth, Nicholas's brother George died and their younger brother <a href="/wiki/Michael_Alexandrovich" class="mw-redirect" title="Michael Alexandrovich">Michael</a> became the heir presumptive to the throne. Courtiers flocked to Michael and treated him as the heir apparent, which distressed Alexandra. In October 1900, Nicholas became ill with abdominal typhus and was confined to bedrest for five weeks. The cabinet were forced to discuss what would happen if Nicholas would die. Alexandra was pregnant with Anastasia, and she insisted that she be named regent in the hope that she would bear a son. However, Nicholas's ministers refused: If Nicholas died, Michael would become tsar. If Alexandra's baby was a boy, Michael would renounce the throne in his nephew's favor. Alexandra was not satisfied, and she grew to distrust Nicholas's ministers for trying to "steal" her future son's inheritance.
</p><p>On 18 June 1901, Alexandra gave birth to <a href="/wiki/Grand_Duchess_Anastasia_Nikolaevna_of_Russia" title="Grand Duchess Anastasia Nikolaevna of Russia">Anastasia</a>. Nicholas's sister, Grand Duchess Xenia, exclaimed, "My God! What a disappointment!… a fourth girl!"<sup id="cite_ref-81" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-81">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup> The French diplomat Maurice Paléologue reported: "The German [Alexandra] has the evil eye. Thanks to her nefarious influence our Emperor is doomed to catastrophe."<sup id="cite_ref-82" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-82">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup> The Russian peasants decided that "the Empress was not beloved in heaven or she would have borne a son."<sup id="cite_ref-83" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-83">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Alexandra and Nicholas turned to the faith in hopes of having a son. Shortly after Anastasia's birth, <a href="/wiki/Princess_Milica_of_Montenegro" title="Princess Milica of Montenegro">Grand Duchess Militza Nikolaevna</a> introduced Alexandra to a mystic named <a href="/wiki/Nizier_Anthelme_Philippe" title="Nizier Anthelme Philippe">Philippe Nizier-Vachot</a>. He was an unlicensed quack who claimed that he could use his magnetic powers to change the sex of a baby inside the womb.<sup id="cite_ref-84" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-84">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup> Nicholas contrived a medical diploma from the Petersburg Military Medical Academy for Philippe and made him State Councilor and military doctor. Nicholas's mother (Marie), sister (Xenia), and sister-in-law (Ella) were alarmed and warned him and Alexandra to stay away from Philippe, but the Imperial couple did not heed their advice.
</p><p>In the end of 1901, Alexandra seemed to have become pregnant again, and Philippe swore that she was carrying a boy. By the summer of 1902, it was clear that the Empress was not pregnant. <a href="/wiki/Grand_Duke_Constantine_Constantinovich_of_Russia" class="mw-redirect" title="Grand Duke Constantine Constantinovich of Russia">Grand Duke Constantine Constantinovich of Russia</a> wrote, "From 8 August we have been waiting every day for confirmation of the Empress's pregnancy. Now we have suddenly learned that she is not pregnant, indeed that there never was any pregnancy, and that the symptoms that led to suppose it were in fact only anaemia!".<sup id="cite_ref-Helen_Rappaport_p._66_85-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Helen_Rappaport_p._66-85">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup> In reality, Alexandra had had a <a href="/wiki/Molar_pregnancy" title="Molar pregnancy">molar pregnancy</a>. On 19 August 1902, she had suffered a discharge of "a spherical, fleshy mass the size of a walnut",<sup id="cite_ref-Helen_Rappaport_p._66_85-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Helen_Rappaport_p._66-85">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup> which Dr. Ott confirmed was a dead fertilized egg in the fourth week of gestation. To save face, the court physicians published a bulletin on 21 August claiming that Alexandra had "a straightforward miscarriage, without any complications."<sup id="cite_ref-86" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-86">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup> Humiliated, Alexandra sent Philippe to France.
</p><p>In 1903, Alexandra and Nicholas decided to support the canonisation of <a href="/wiki/Seraphim_of_Sarov" title="Seraphim of Sarov">Seraphim of Sarov</a>. Before he left Russia, Philippe told them that Seraphim would grant Alexandra a son. Seraphim was a monk in the Tambov region who had supposedly performed local miracles, he had been dead for seventy years. The Metropolitan of Moscow reluctantly agreed to canonize the saint. On 19 August, Alexandra and Nicholas bathed in the Sarov Spring in which Seraphim had once bathed and prayed that the <a href="/wiki/Sacred_waters" title="Sacred waters">sacred waters</a> would bless them with a son.<sup id="cite_ref-87" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-87">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1904, Alexandra became pregnant. There was high anticipation for a son. As her due date drew near, a newspaper noted that "a few days will decide whether the Czarina is to be the most popular woman in Russia, or regarded by the great bulk of the people as a castaway – under the special wrath of God."<sup id="cite_ref-88" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-88">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup> On 12 August 1904, Alexandra gave birth to <a href="/wiki/Alexei_Nikolaevich,_Tsarevich_of_Russia" title="Alexei Nikolaevich, Tsarevich of Russia">Alexei Nikolaevich</a> in <a href="/wiki/Petergof" title="Petergof">Peterhof</a>. Alexei's birth affirmed Nicholas and Alexandra's faith in Philippe. In her diary, Nicholas's sister Olga wrote, "I am sure it was Seraphim who brought it about." Nicholas wrote to Militza to "pass on our gratitude and joy … to Philippe."<sup id="cite_ref-89" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-89">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Relationship_with_her_children">Relationship with her children</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Alexandra_Feodorovna_(Alix_of_Hesse)&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Relationship with her children">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Family_Nicholas_II_of_Russia_ca._1914.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8e/Family_Nicholas_II_of_Russia_ca._1914.jpg/250px-Family_Nicholas_II_of_Russia_ca._1914.jpg" decoding="async" width="250" height="203" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8e/Family_Nicholas_II_of_Russia_ca._1914.jpg/375px-Family_Nicholas_II_of_Russia_ca._1914.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8e/Family_Nicholas_II_of_Russia_ca._1914.jpg/500px-Family_Nicholas_II_of_Russia_ca._1914.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4295" data-file-height="3495" /></a><figcaption>The Russian Imperial Family, 1913. Left to right: Grand Duchess Maria, Tsarina Alexandra, Grand Duchesses Olga and Tatiana, Tsar Nicholas II, and Grand Duchess Anastasia. Tsesarevich Alexei sits in front of his parents.</figcaption></figure>
<p>Alexandra had a distant relationship with Olga.<sup id="cite_ref-Massie,_R_p.154_90-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Massie,_R_p.154-90">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup> She relied on Olga to keep her younger siblings in order. Her letters to Olga include frequent reminders to mind her siblings: "Remember above all to always be a good example to the little ones"<sup id="cite_ref-91" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-91">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup> and "Try to have a serious word with Tatiana and Maria about how they should conduct themselves towards God.<sup id="cite_ref-92" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-92">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup>" Olga was frustrated by trying to keep her boisterous siblings in order, and she complained that her mother had no time for her. Olga preferred her father.
</p><p>Alexandra was closest to her second daughter, Tatiana.<sup id="cite_ref-93" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-93">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup> Tatiana resembled Alexandra the most in terms of appearance and personality. She was described by her paternal aunt Xenia: "[Tatiana] and her mother are like as two peas in a pod!.... so pretty."<sup id="cite_ref-94" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-94">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup> She was cautious and reserved, and she was unquestioningly devoted to Alexandra. During the family's final months, she helped her mother by pushing her about the house in a wheelchair.
</p><p>Maria felt insecure about her role in the family, and Alexandra frequently assured Maria that she was as loved as her siblings: "Sweet child you must promise me never again to think that nobody loves you. How did such an extraordinary idea get into your little head? Get it quickly out again." Maria worried that Alexandra favored Anastasia over her, and Alexandra reassured her that "I have no secrets with Anastasia."<sup id="cite_ref-95" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-95">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Anastasia physically resembled Alexandra, but her boisterous, mischievous personality was very different from her mother's. She was dubbed the <i>shvibzik,</i> Russian for "imp."<sup id="cite_ref-96" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-96">&#91;96&#93;</a></sup> During the family's last months, Anastasia was the only one who could make the melancholy Alexandra laugh.
</p><p>Alexandra doted on Alexei because he was her only son and the heir to the Russian Empire. The children's tutor <a href="/wiki/Pierre_Gilliard" title="Pierre Gilliard">Pierre Gilliard</a> wrote, "Alexei was the centre of a united family, the focus of all its hopes and affections. His sisters worshipped him. He was his parents' pride and joy. When he was well, the palace was transformed. Everyone and everything in it seemed bathed in sunshine."<sup id="cite_ref-Massie,_R_p.158_97-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Massie,_R_p.158-97">&#91;97&#93;</a></sup> Alexandra was obsessed with trying to protect him from his disease of hemophilia. According to Gilliard, she "press[ed] the little boy to her with the convulsive movement of a mother who always seems in fear of her child's life."<sup id="cite_ref-98" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-98">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup> She sat at Alexei's bedside for days as he suffered through his fatal attacks. She feared that he would injure himself in tantrums, so she spoiled him and never punished him.
</p><p>Despite her fears of never bearing a son, Alexandra loved her daughters and called them her "little four-leaved clover." She wrote that "our girlies are our joy and happiness" and "the apostles of God."<sup id="cite_ref-99" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-99">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Health">Health</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Alexandra_Feodorovna_(Alix_of_Hesse)&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Health">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Alexandra's health was never robust and her frequent pregnancies, with four daughters in six years and her son three years after, drew from her energy. Her biographers, including Robert Massie, Carrolly Erickson, Greg King, and Peter Kurth, attribute the semi-invalidism of her later years to nervous exhaustion from obsessive worry over the fragile tsarevich, who suffered from hemophilia. She spent most of her time in bed or reclining on a chaise in her boudoir or on a veranda. This immobility enabled her to avoid the social occasions that she found distasteful. Alexandra regularly took a herbal medicine known as <a href="/wiki/Adonis_vernalis" title="Adonis vernalis">adonis vernalis</a> in order to regulate her pulse. She was constantly tired, slept badly, and complained of swollen feet. She ate little, but never lost weight (except for the last year of her life). She may have suffered from <a href="/wiki/Graves_disease" class="mw-redirect" title="Graves disease">Graves disease</a> (<a href="/wiki/Hyperthyroidism" title="Hyperthyroidism">hyperthyroidism</a>), a condition resulting in high levels of the thyroid hormone, which can also result in <a href="/wiki/Atrial_fibrillation" title="Atrial fibrillation">atrial fibrillation</a>, poor heartbeat and lack of energy.<sup id="cite_ref-100" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-100">&#91;100&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Haemophilia_and_Rasputin">Haemophilia and Rasputin</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Alexandra_Feodorovna_(Alix_of_Hesse)&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Haemophilia and Rasputin">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Alexandra_Feodorovna_with_her_son.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/60/Alexandra_Feodorovna_with_her_son.jpg/180px-Alexandra_Feodorovna_with_her_son.jpg" decoding="async" width="180" height="184" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/60/Alexandra_Feodorovna_with_her_son.jpg/270px-Alexandra_Feodorovna_with_her_son.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/60/Alexandra_Feodorovna_with_her_son.jpg/360px-Alexandra_Feodorovna_with_her_son.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2447" data-file-height="2497" /></a><figcaption>Alexandra with her son, Alexei, 1913</figcaption></figure>
